Question 1 (ID: 1):
The SI standard of time is based on:

Options:
  A) the daily rotation of the earth
  B) the frequency of light emitted by Kr86
  C) the yearly revolution of the earth about the sun
  D) a precision pendulum clock
  E) none of these [CORRECT ANSWER]

Verified Answer: none of these

-------------------------------------------------------

Question 2 (ID: 2):
A nanosecond is:

Options:
  A) 109 s
  B) 10−9 s [CORRECT ANSWER]
  C) 10−10 s
  D) 10−12

Verified Answer: 10−9 s

-------------------------------------------------------

Question 3 (ID: 3):
The SI standard of length is based on:

Options:
  A) the distance from the north pole to the equator along a meridian passing through Paris
  B) wavelength of light emitted by Hg198
  C) wavelength of light emitted by Kr86
  D) a precision meter stick in Paris
  E) the speed of light [CORRECT ANSWER]

Verified Answer: the speed of light

-------------------------------------------------------

Question 4 (ID: 4):
In 1866, the U. S. Congress defined the U. S. yard as exactly 3600/3937 international meter. This was done primarily because:

Options:
  A) length can be measured more accurately in meters than in yards
  B) the meter is more stable than the yard
  C) this definition relates the common U. S. length units to a more widely used system [CORRECT ANSWER]
  D) there are more wavelengths in a yard than in a meter
  E) the members of this Congress were exceptionally intelligent

Verified Answer: this definition relates the common U. S. length units to a more widely used system

-------------------------------------------------------

  D) 100 m
  E) 1000 m

Verified Answer: 1 m

Question 6 (ID: 6):
There is no SI base unit for area because:

Options:

  B) we live in a three (not a two) dimensional world
  C) it is impossible to express square feet in terms of meters
  D) area can be expressed in terms of square meters [CORRECT ANSWER]
  E) area is not an important physical quantity

Question 26 (ID: 26):

Options:
  A) xi = 4 m, xf = 6 m
  B) xi = −4 m, xf = −8 m
  C) xi = −4 m, xf = 2 m
  D) xi = 4 m, xf = −2 m
  E) xi = −4 m, xf = 4 m [CORRECT ANSWER]

Verified Answer: xi = −4 m, xf = 4 m

Question 27 (ID: 27):
The average speed of a moving object during a given interval of time is always:

Options:
  A) the magnitude of its average velocity over the interval
  B) the distance covered during the time interval divided by the time interval [CORRECT ANSWER]
  C) one-half its speed at the end of the interval
  D) its acceleration multiplied by the time interval
  E) one-half its acceleration multiplied by the time interval.

Verified Answer: the distance covered during the time interval divided by the time interval

Question 61 (ID: 61):

Options:

  B) During each second the object falls 9.8 m
  C) The acceleration changes by 9.8 m/s2 every second
  D) The object falls 9.8 m during the first second of time

Question 62 (ID: 62):

Options:
  A) the body falls 9.8 m during each second
  B) the body falls 9.8 m during the first second only
  C) the speed of the body increases by 9.8 m/s during each second [CORRECT ANSWER]
  D) the acceleration of the body increases by 9.8 m/s2 during each second
  E) the acceleration of the body decreases by 9.8 m/s2 during each second

Verified Answer: the speed of the body increases by 9.8 m/s during each second

Options:
  A) applied tangentially halfway between the axis and the rim
  B) applied tangentially at the rim [CORRECT ANSWER]
  C) applied radially halfway between the axis and the rim
  D) applied radially at the rim

A block of mass m1 is attached to one end and a block of mass m2 is attached to the other. At one time the block with mass m1 is moving downward with speed v. If the string does not slip on the pulley, the magnitude of the total angular momentum, about the pulley center, of the blocks and pulley, considered as a system, is given by:

Options:
  A) (m1 − m2 )vR + Iv/R
  B) (m1 + m2 )vR + Iv/R [CORRECT ANSWER]
  C) (m1 − m2 )vR + Iv/R2
  D) (m1 + m2 )vR + Iv/R2
  E) none of the above

Verified Answer: (m1 + m2 )vR + Iv/R

-------------------------------------------------------

  D) in the negative x direction

  B) is impossible by second law
  C) would only work if the ground and the house were at the same temperature
  D) is impossible since heat energy flows from the (hot) house to the (cold) ground
  E) is possible [CORRECT ANSWER]

Verified Answer: is possible

  C) on the line joining Q and −Q, to the side of Q opposite −Q
  D) on the line joining Q and −Q, to the side of −Q opposite Q
  E) at none of these places (there is no place) [CORRECT ANSWER]

Verified Answer: at none of these places (there is no place)
